It taught him that no letter from Verschoyle to Peters need at present be expected. It taught him that a letter from Bryanston, which must be expected, might be dangerous and must be intercepted. It taught him that Peters would remain inactive only till that letter reached his hands. It taught him also that if Peters were put to silence, Kuyper, the other European who had seen that body in Sulu, could tell no tales.

===Chinese===
到这一步，塔尔贝格先生骤然全军倒戈——连人带马带炮——投向了敌方。他向卡拉汉保证，凡他垂询有关他与韦恩-卡特赖特交往的任何问题，他都乐于和盘托出；如蒙俯允，他还愿把这段交往的由来从头讲起。

事情归结起来是这样。塔尔贝格早年与一位律师合伙开业，那位律师是朗赫斯特的代理律师。1882年初，朗赫斯特在英格兰盘桓了一个月，其间为几桩烦心的事咨询过塔尔贝格的合伙人，事情同他与韦恩-卡特赖特的合伙有关。朗赫斯特究竟向他的合伙人陈诉了什么具体的委屈，塔尔贝格记不得了（至少他是这么说的），只知道事涉韦恩-卡特赖特为自己谋得了特许权、置下了产业，而朗赫斯特认为——照塔尔贝格的料想，这认为并无根据——这些本应归入合伙生意。人家给朗赫斯特出的什么主意，塔尔贝格也无从知晓。此后，除了他溺水身亡的一句传闻，他再没听到朗赫斯特的音讯；直到他死后，塔尔贝格先前素不相识的韦恩-卡特赖特来到伦敦，雇请这家事务所寻访朗赫斯特家族中尚在人世的几位成员——对这些人，他如今表现得极为慷慨。自那以后，正如我们所知，塔尔贝格担任了韦恩-卡特赖特创办的一家公司的法律顾问，间或也为他办些绝无惊险可言的私人法律事务。可是到了去年，也就是1896年1月中旬，韦恩-卡特赖特命塔尔贝格以最隐秘的方式替他进行几项查询。一项是向巴格达的一个人，查问某位布莱恩斯顿先生的身份与来历；一项关乎巴达维亚的一位医生兼科学家凯珀尔博士——经查明，此人已经亡故。另一项，如卡拉汉所知，是发给马德里的一位通信人；但塔尔贝格声明，这项查询仅止于查明当时在菲律宾担任检察总长——抑或我记得是司法部长——之职的那人的姓名住址。我冒昧请问了那姓名；那名字，我在彼得斯的那些笔记里早先就见过。最后一项，是关于弗肖伊尔博士的查询：塔尔贝格奉命在某个日期之前，设法与此人会上一面。他声称，会面的目的，是向他收取一些笔记与日记，供在福音传播协会（Society for the Propagation of the Gospel）主持下于菲律宾新设一处传教站之用；他说，韦恩-卡特赖特似乎对这桩事业兴致极浓（而事实上，他先前也确实捐助过传教团体）。塔尔贝格这趟朗威尔顿之行，缘由便在于此：他奉命务必在他实际抵达的那一日赶到那里，把查询所得的结果——如果有的话——当面口告韦恩-卡特赖特。那结果，简而言之是：布莱恩斯顿就是当年在长崎待过的那个人；凯珀尔已死；马尼拉的司法部长（或不论那官职确切叫什么）便是前文提到的那位；至于弗肖伊尔，身在国外，不久前还在锡耶纳，却于几周前突然离去——据信是去了德国，但没有留下任何地址。这一切，塔尔贝格都在凶案发生前一天的下午，于彼得斯宅中一一禀报了韦恩-卡特赖特。而这一切教给韦恩-卡特赖特的，虽有几分晦暗，也有一部分明摆着。它教给他：眼下不必指望弗肖伊尔会有信写给彼得斯。它教给他：布莱恩斯顿的信必来，来了便可能危险，必须中途截下。它教给他：彼得斯按兵不动，只捱得到那封信落入他手中为止。它还教给他：只要彼得斯永远缄口，另一个在苏禄见过那具尸身的欧洲人凯珀尔，也就再讲不出什么故事了。

===Original===
After Peters’ death, Thalberg, still acting under instructions, had had an interview with Dr. Verschoyle at Homburg, to which he had traced him, and had taken with him a letter written on the paper of the S.P.G., and signed, as he believed, by the secretary of that society. (It has since appeared that the secretary had no knowledge of such a letter.) Dr. Verschoyle delivered to him some journals which he, Thalberg, never read, for transmission to Vane-Cartwright, to whom he duly delivered them. That, he said, was all that he knew of the subjects on which Callaghan sought information. He denied all knowledge of further communications made on behalf of Vane-Cartwright with that important official in the Philippines; but he appeared to me somewhat nervous in answering Callaghan’s questions on this matter, and anxious to appease him with the prospect that he might be able, through friends of his, to ascertain what communications of this nature had actually taken place.

It was curious to how many questions suggested to us by what he had said he could give no answer. Indeed he informed us, with an air of moral self-complacency, that he thought it a very sound maxim for a professional man to know as little as possible of things which it was not his business to know. I guessed that perhaps his strict observance of this precept was the thing which had commended him to the service of Vane-Cartwright, but I really do believe that Mr. Thalberg knew nothing behind the facts which he now thought it convenient to himself to reveal.

===Chinese===
彼得斯死后，塔尔贝格仍奉命行事，在霍姆堡循迹找到了弗肖伊尔博士，同他会了一面；随身携去一封写在福音传播协会（S.P.G.）信纸上的信，据他信，署名人是协会干事。（后来查明，那位干事对这样一封信毫不知情。）弗肖伊尔博士交给他几册日记，他塔尔贝格从未读过，只为转呈韦恩-卡特赖特；他也如期呈交了。他说，关于卡拉汉想打听的那些事，他所知道的尽在于此。至于韦恩-卡特赖特同菲律宾那位要员之间进一步的往来，他一概推作不知；但在回答卡拉汉就此的盘问时，他看去未免心虚，又急欲安抚卡拉汉，指望或可借他的一些朋友之力，查明这一类往来究竟有过哪些。

奇怪的是，他这番话在我们心里引出多少疑问，他就有多少答不上来。他甚至还带着一股道德上的自满告诉我们：依他看，一个吃专业饭的人，对本分以外的事知道得越少越好，这是一条十分牢靠的箴言。我暗自揣度，或许正是他对这条箴言的恪守，使他入了韦恩-卡特赖特的法眼，得供驱策；不过我真心相信，在如今这些他觉得交代出来于己方便的事实背后，塔尔贝格先生确实别无所知。

===Original===
However that may be, he made no secret of anything which he could disclose without injury to himself. We had got from him, or I ought to say Callaghan had got from him, evidence which might serve to show plainly enough that Vane-Cartwright was aware of Peters’ suspicions and concerned himself greatly about them, and, content with this, we were preparing to go when Mr. Thalberg stopped us saying that there was one important matter of which we had not asked him yet, and perhaps should be surprised to know that he could tell us anything. I have omitted to say that in the course of the conversation he had heard something from us about the things which had led to Vane-Cartwright’s being suspected. We had told him in substance the story about the tracks, and were much surprised to find that he appeared wholly ignorant of the charge that had been brought against Trethewy. He now told us a fact which had a great bearing upon the history of those tracks. He asked us whether or not Peters’ grounds could be seen from the upper rooms of the hotel. I said that no doubt they could, for the hotel was only too visible from those grounds. He then stated that having confined himself to his bedroom until it was time for him to start for his train, he had at a certain hour noticed a man walking across Peters’ field (for from his description it was plain to me that it was Peters’ field, and plain further that the man was walking pretty much where those tracks were made). This man, even at that distance, he recognised as Vane-Cartwright; he recognised him by his fur coat and a cap which Ellen Trethewy had seen him in, and by some peculiarity about his gait which he knew well. The man was also swinging his stick in Vane-Cartwright’s own particular manner. The distance was considerable, but I knew that it would be possible for a clear-sighted man to recognise at that distance anyone whom he knew very well. The hour which Thalberg named corresponded with what Ellen Trethewy had told me.

===Chinese===
无论怎样，凡于自己无损而能披露的事，他都毫不隐讳。我们从他手里——或者说，我该说卡拉汉从他手里——取得了足以清楚表明这层的证据：韦恩-卡特赖特早知彼得斯起了疑心，且为此极是挂怀。我们心满意足，正要起身告辞，塔尔贝格先生却拦住我们，说还有一桩要紧事我们不曾问起，而他竟能就此告诉我们一些东西，我们听了怕要吃惊。我一直漏说了一事：谈话之间，他已从我们口中听到些许导致韦恩-卡特赖特招疑的情由。我们把雪地足迹的始末大略讲了给他，却大为诧异地发现，对加在特雷瑟维头上的那桩指控，他竟似乎全然无知。这时他告诉我们一个事实，同那些足迹的来龙去脉干系甚大。他问我们，从旅馆楼上的房间里，可望得见彼得斯宅的园地。我说无疑望得见，因为从那片园地里望去，那家旅馆实在太扎眼。他于是陈述道：动身赶火车之前，他一直蛰居在自己卧室里，其间在某个钟点，望见一个人正徒步穿过彼得斯的那块田地（听他的描述，我当即明白那必是彼得斯的田地；更明白，那人行走的路线，与留下那些足迹之处大致不差）。那个人，隔着那样远，他认出正是韦恩-卡特赖特；他是凭那件毛皮大衣和一顶帽子认出的——埃伦·特雷瑟维见过他戴——又凭一种他素来熟稔的步态特异之处。那人挥动手杖的姿式，也是韦恩-卡特赖特独有的做派。距离着实不近，但我知道，一个目光锐利的人，隔着那段路认出一个与他极熟的人，是办得到的。塔尔贝格报出的那个钟点，同埃伦·特雷瑟维对我讲的正相吻合。

## XXI

===Original===
As we left Thalberg’s office and walked down the narrow court which led to the street, I daresay our looks and voices, if not our words, betrayed the exultation of men who see a long-sought object at last within reach. As we turned into the street we were stopped by Vane-Cartwright.

Only the day before I had been expecting to find him lurking for me round every corner; but now and here it startled me to meet him. When I learnt why he met us, it startled me still more, and looking back upon it, I still find it unaccountable.

“Mr. Driver, Mr. Callaghan,” he said, addressing us in turn in tones as quiet as ever, but with a pale face and highly-strung manner, “I am your prisoner.” I suppose we stared for a moment, for he repeated, “I am your prisoner. I will go with you where you like; or you can give me in charge to the nearest constable. There is one. You see you have beaten me. You probably do not yet know it yourselves, but you have.”
===Chinese===
我们离开塔尔贝格的事务所，沿那条通往大街的窄巷走去。我想，纵然言语不露声色，我们的神情和语调也准会泄露心头的狂喜——苦苦追寻的目标终于近在咫尺的人，正是这副样子。刚拐上大街，韦恩-卡特赖特便拦住了我们。

就在前一天，我还料想他会埋伏在每个街角守着我；可此时此地撞见他，我却吃了一惊。待到得知他为何而来，我更是惊愕不已；如今回望这一幕，我仍旧觉得不可思议。

「德赖弗先生，卡拉汉先生，」他挨个称呼我们，声调一如既往地平静，脸色却苍白，神经过分紧绷，「我是你们的俘虏。」我想我们定是怔了片刻，因为他又重复了一遍：「我是你们的俘虏。你们乐意带我上哪儿，我就上哪儿；要不然，你们可以把我扭送给最近的警员。那边就有一位。你们瞧，你们已经赢了我。你们自己多半还没有意识到，可你们确实是赢了。」

===Original===
“Well,” he continued, “if you do not quite know what you are going to do, I will ask one thing of you. Before you give me up to justice, take me somewhere where I can talk with you two alone. I want to tell you my story. It will not make you alter your purpose, I know that; but it will make you respect me a little more than you do. It is odd that I should want that, but I do.”

“Well, gentlemen?” he said questioningly, as we still hesitated, and his old self-possession returning for a moment, a smile of positive amusement came over his face.

I confess that if I had acted on my own impulse I should have taken my antagonist at his word when he suggested that we should call the nearest policeman. But Callaghan had been taking the lead in our late movements, and I felt that the occasion belonged to Callaghan; and Callaghan was more generous.
===Chinese===
「这样吧，」他接着说，「如果你们还没完全拿定主意怎么处置我，我想求你们一件事。在把我交给司法之前，带我去一个能同你们两位单独谈话的地方。我想把我的故事讲给你们听。我知道，这不会让你们改变主意；但它会让你们对我的敬意，比现在多上一点。说来奇怪，我竟然想要这个，可我确实想要。」

「怎么样，两位先生？」见我们仍在迟疑，他探询地问。往日那副从容自若一时间回到了他身上，他脸上竟然当真浮现出觉得有趣的微笑。

我承认，倘若依着自己的性子，他既提议我们去叫最近的警察，我便照办不误了。但近来的一举一动一直是卡拉汉在领头，我觉得这个场合理应由卡拉汉做主；而卡拉汉比我宽厚。

===Original===
“If you have anything to say, sir,” he said, “come to my chambers and say it. Four-wheeler!”

In a moment more we were in a cab﻿—how slow the cab seemed﻿—Callaghan sitting opposite Vane-Cartwright and watching him narrowly lest he should play us a trick, while I too watched him all through the interminable drive, very ill at ease as to the wisdom of our conduct, and wondering what could be the meaning of the unexpected and desperate hazard which our antagonist was now taking. He was evidently going to confess to us. But why? If the knowledge we already possessed was sufficient, as perhaps it was, to secure his conviction, yet he could only partly guess what that knowledge was; of the two most telling pieces of evidence against him, the fact about the window-latch which the surgeon had told us, and the fact that Thalberg had recognised him afar from his window in the hotel, he must have been quite unaware. And then what did he expect to gain by the interview which he had sought with us? What opinion had he formed of the mental weaknesses of the two men with whom he was playing? Was he relying overmuch upon the skill and mastery of himself and others which he would bring to bear in this strange interview? Had the fearful strain under which he had been living of late taken away the coolness and acuteness of his judgment? Could he rely so much upon the chance of enlisting our compassion that he could afford to give us a certainty of his guilt, which, for all he knew, we had not got before, and to throw away the hope of making an escape by flight, which with a man of his resource might easily have been successful? Or had he some other far more sinister hope than that of stirring us to unworthy pity or generosity? I could not resolve these questions, but I was inclined to an explanation which he was himself about to give us. If the cause of suspicion against him became public he would have lost everything for which he greatly cared, and he was ready to risk all upon any chance, however faint, of avoiding this. I was, as I have said, ill at ease about it all. I did not feel that after the conversation I had held with him before, Vane-Cartwright would get over me, but it is an experience which one would do much to avoid, that of listening obdurate to an appeal into which another man puts his whole heart; and more especially would one wish to have avoided consenting to hear that appeal in a manner which might raise false hopes. But for a more serious reason it had been a mistake to acquiesce in this interview; I had learned to know not only Callaghan’s goodness of heart but his cleverness and his promptitude, but I had not learned to credit him with wisdom or with firmness; and the sort of impulsiveness, which had made him at once grant the request for this interview, might easily have further and graver consequences.
===Chinese===
「先生，你要有什么话要说，」他说，「就到我寓所去说。四轮马车！」

不一会儿，我们便坐进了一辆马车——那马车走得多慢啊——卡拉汉坐在韦恩-卡特赖特对面，紧紧盯住他，唯恐他耍什么花招；在那段漫无尽头的车程里，我也一直注视着他，心里对我们此举是否明智深感不安，暗自纳罕：我们的对手此刻行此出乎意料的孤注一掷，究竟意欲何为。他显然是要向我们坦白了。可为什么呢？我们已掌握的情况——或许已足以给他定罪——他至多只能猜到一部分；而对他最致命的两条证据，一是外科医生告诉我们的窗闩那件事，二是塔尔贝格在旅馆里从他房间的窗口远远认出他一事，他必定毫不知情。那么，他主动谋求的这场会面，指望从中得到什么？他把我们这两个被他玩弄于股掌之上的人的心性弱点，估成了什么样子？他是否过分倚仗自己的手段，倚仗掌控自身与他人的本领，以为凭这些就足以应付这场离奇的会面？他近来承受的那可怕的重压，是否已磨去了他判断中的冷静与敏锐？他是否把博取我们同情的侥幸押得如此之重，以至于甘愿向我们交出他确凿的罪证——就他所知，我们此前未必已经掌握——并且舍弃出逃的一线生机？以他的神通，若要远走高飞，本是轻而易举。又或者，他怀着的指望，远比撩起我们不够体面的怜悯或宽宏更为阴险？这些问题我解答不了，但倾向于接受他自己即将给出的那种解释：一旦对他的怀疑闹到公开，他最为看重的种种便尽数化为乌有；因此哪怕再渺茫的机会，只要能避免这一结局，他都肯押上全部去赌。如我所说，这一切都令我心神不宁。我倒并不担心，经过先前与他那番谈话之后，韦恩-卡特赖特还能把我争取过去；可是，听一个人倾注全副心血地恳求，而自己却硬起心肠不为所动——这种滋味，谁都想极力避开；尤其是，当初竟以某种方式答应了听这番恳求，而那方式说不定会让他生出虚妄的希望，这一点更叫人悔之不及。但默许这场会面终究是个错误，理由还更严重些：我固然领教了卡拉汉的心地之善，也领教了他的精明果断，却从未见他有过什么审慎或坚定；而那种使他一口应承下这场会面的冲动习性，很容易招致更深远、更严重的后果。

===Original===
At last we were in Callaghan’s room and seated ourselves round a table.

“I see,” said Vane-Cartwright, “that it puzzles you gentlemen why I should ask for this interview. You think I am an ordinary criminal, which perhaps I am, and you thought that like an ordinary criminal I should try all means to save a disgraced life, which I certainly shall not do. I know that you have not got the knowledge which would convict me of murder. I do not suppose you think you have, and in any case you have not. And, if you had, I think you know I have contrivance enough to take myself off and live comfortably out of reach of the law. But I do not care for escape, and I do not care for acquittal. You have the means to throw suspicion on me, and that is enough for me. I cared for honour and success, and I do not care for life when they are lost.” He was looking at each of us alternately with an inscrutable but quite unflinching gaze, but he now hid his eyes, and he added as if with difficulty, “Yet I did care for one other thing besides my position in the world, but that has gone from me too.
===Chinese===
我们终于到了卡拉汉的寓所，围着一张桌子坐了下来。

「我明白，」韦恩-卡特赖特说，「二位先生觉得纳闷，我为什么要请求这次会面。你们以为我是个寻常罪犯——也许我本来就是；你们以为我会像寻常罪犯那样，使尽一切手段保全这条已然蒙羞的性命——这我断然不会。我知道，你们并没有掌握足以给我定谋杀罪的证据。我想你们自己也并不以为有；无论如何，你们确实没有。退一步说，就算你们有，我想你们也清楚，我有足够的手段脱身而去，舒舒服服地活在法律够不着的地方。可我不在乎逃亡，也不在乎脱罪。你们有办法把嫌疑压到我头上，这对我来说就足够了。我从前看重的是体面和功业；如今这两样都完了，性命我也就不放在心上了。」他交替注视着我们两人，目光莫测，却毫无畏缩；此刻他把眼睛垂了下去，艰难似的补了一句：「不过，除了我在世间的地位，我确实还看重过另一样东西——可它也已经离我而去了。

===Original===
“And now,” he resumed, “that my struggle is over, and that the people﻿—more people and bigger people than you would think﻿—who have been courting me for the last twelve months will think of me only with as just abhorrence as Thalberg himself does, I have an odd fancy, and it is this: I should like to stand a little better in the eyes of the very men who, far from courting me, have had the courage to suspect me and the tenacity to drag me down.” He had raised his eyes again, but this time fixed them on Callaghan only, for he doubtless saw that I was out of touch with him, and, seeing this, he had art enough to appear to recognise and acquiesce in it.
===Chinese===
「如今，」他重新开口，「我的挣扎已经到头了；过去十二个月里一直逢迎我的那些人——人数之多、来头之大，远超你们的想象——往后想到我时，将只剩下恰如其分的憎恶，一如塔尔贝格本人对我的憎恶。这时我心里生出一个古怪的念头，就是这样：我盼着能在另外几个人眼里，把自己的形象稍稍挽回一些——恰恰是这几个人，非但从不逢迎我，还有胆量怀疑我，有韧性把我拉下马来。」他又抬起了眼睛，但这一次只望着卡拉汉一个人，因为他无疑看出我与他格格不入；而既已看出，他也有足够的手段，装出承认并接受这一点的样子。

===Original===
“You know something of my story. Let me tell you just a little more of it, and, please, if it interests you enough, question me on any point you will. I shall not shrink from answering. If a man is known to have murdered two of his friends, there cannot be much left that it is worth his while to conceal. First, I would like to speak of my early training. If I had been brought up in the gutter, you could make some allowance for that, and give me some credit for any good qualities I had shown, however cheerfully you might see me hanged for my crimes. It is not usual to suppose that any such allowance may have to be made for a man brought up to luxury and to every sort of refinement, and yet such a man too may be the victim of influences which would kill the good in most characters even more than they have in mine. You may have heard a little about my people, and perhaps know that their views and ways were not quite usual; I am not going to say one word against them (I am not that sort of man, whatever I may be), but there were two things in my boyhood harder for me than the ordinary Englishman can well imagine. I was brought up in the actual enjoyment of considerable wealth and the expectation of really great wealth, and just when I was grown up the wealth and the expectations suddenly vanished. That has happened to many men who have been none the worse for it. But then I was brought up soft. You know I am not a limp man or a coward; but I had all the bringing up of one; cared for hand and foot, never doing a thing for myself (my good people had great ideas of republican simplicity, but they were only literary ideas). None of the games, none of the sport that other boys get; no rubbing shoulders with my equals at school; no comradeship but only the company of my elders, mostly invalids. Few people know what it is to be brought up soft. But there was worse than that. You” (he was addressing Callaghan) “were piously brought up. Oh, yes, you were really. I daresay your home was not a strict one, and you were not carefully taught precepts of religion and morality or carefully shielded from the sight of evil (perhaps quite the contrary, for I have not the pleasure of knowing much about you, Mr. Callaghan), but I am quite sure that you had about you at home or at school, or both, people among whom there was some tacit recognition of right and wrong of some sort as things incontrovertible, and that there was some influence in your childhood which appealed to the heart. But in my childhood nothing appealed to the heart, nothing was incontrovertible, above all, nothing was tacit. Everlasting discussion, reaching back to the first principles of the universe, and branching out into such questions as whether children should be allowed popguns. That was my moral training, and that was all my moral training. It was very sound in principle, I daresay﻿—and I am not going to pose as an interesting convert to the religious way of looking at things, for I am not one﻿—but it did not take account of practical difficulties, and it was very, very hard on me. Not one man in ten thousand has had that sort of upbringing, and I do not suppose you can realise in the least how hard that sort of thing is.
===Chinese===
「你们已经知道我经历的一部分。容我再稍作补充；而且，倘若你们听得起了兴致，请随意就任何一点盘问，我绝不回避作答。一个众所周知谋害过两个朋友的人，值得遮掩的事也就不剩什么了。首先，我想谈谈我早年的教养。倘若我是在阴沟里长大的，你们可以为此体谅几分，我身上显露的任何可取之处，你们也肯记我几分功劳——哪怕你们照样乐呵呵地看着我为我的罪案被送上绞架。人们通常不会想到，对一个自幼养尊处优、受尽种种雅致熏陶的人，竟也需要作这样的体谅；然而这样的人，同样可能沦为某些影响的牺牲品——那些影响足以扼杀大多数品性中的善，而且比起在我身上做到的，只会更加彻底。你们或许听说过一些我家人的情形，也许知道他们的观念与做派都颇为异于常人；我不想说他们一句坏话（不论我是什么人，我可不是那种人）；但我少年时代有两件事，其艰难远非寻常的英国人所能想象。其一，我自幼实际坐享着相当的家财，又指望着真正巨额的财富；而我刚刚成年，家财与指望便倏然化为乌有。这种事落在许多人身上，他们并未因此变坏。可还有一层：我是被娇养大的。你们知道，我不是个软骨头，也不是懦夫；可我受的偏偏是软骨头受的教养：被照料得无微不至，凡事不曾亲自动过一桩手（我的好家人对共和式的简朴自有宏论，可惜只是纸上的宏论）。别的男孩都有的游戏、都有的运动，我一概没有；在学校里从不与同龄人摩肩接踵；没有同伴之谊，只有长辈作伴，而长辈多半缠绵病榻。很少有人知道被娇养大是什么滋味。可还有比这更糟的。你」——这话是对卡拉汉说的——「是在虔诚之中长大的。哦，是的，千真万确。我敢说你的家教并不严苛，没有人认真向你讲授宗教与道德的诫训，也没有人刻意护着你不叫你看见恶事（也许恰恰相反，因为卡拉汉先生，我无缘深知你的经历）；但我十分确信，无论在家里还是在学校，或者两处皆是，你身边总有那样一些人，在他们中间，某种是非之别得到了默认，被视为不容置疑的东西；我也确信，你的童年里总有过某种打动人心的感化。可在我的童年，没有什么打动人心，没有什么不容置疑，尤其没有什么是不言自明的。无休无止的讨论，一路追溯到宇宙的终极原理，又旁逸斜出，枝蔓到诸如该不该准孩子玩玩具枪一类的问题。这便是我的道德训练，而且是我全部的道德训练。就原理而言，它想必十分健全——我也不打算把自己装扮成一个幡然归向宗教世界观的有趣改宗者，因为我并非这种人——可它全然不顾实际的重重艰难，而它待我，实在太狠、太狠了。一万人中也无一人受过那种教养；我想，那种教养是何等难熬，你们连一分也想象不出。

===Original===
“So,” he continued, “I found myself at twenty-one suddenly made poor; more accustomed than most lads to think life only worth living for refinements which are for the wealthy only; taught not to take traditional canons of morality for granted; taught to think about the real utility of every action; landed in a place like Saigon, and thrown in the society of the sort of gentry who, we all know, do represent European civilisation in such places; sent there to get a living; thoroughly out of sympathy with all the tastes and pleasures of the people round me, and at the same time easily able to discover that for all my strange upbringing I was by nature more of a man than anyone else there. As a matter of fact, there was only one decent man there with intellectual tastes, and that was Peters; but Peters, who was only two or three years older than I, and, as I own I fancied, nothing like so clever, took me under his protection and made it his mission to correct me, and it did not do. You can easily imagine how, in the three years before Longhurst came on the scene, I had got to hate the prospect of a life of humdrum, money-grubbing among those people in the hope of retiring with a small competence some day when my liver and my brain were gone; you would not have thought any the better of me if I had become content with that. At any rate I did not. I meant to be quit of it as soon as I could, and I meant more. I resolved before I had been three weeks in the place to make money on a scale which would give me the position, the society and the pursuits for which I had been trained. I resolved in fact to make the sort of place for myself in the world which every man, except the three men in this room and Thalberg, thinks I have secured. If I had no scruples as to the way in which I should carry out that resolve, I differed from the people around me only in knowing that I had no scruples, and in having instead a set purpose which I was man enough to pursue through life. And I am man enough, I hope, not to care much for life now that that purpose has failed. If I pursued my end without scruple, I think I was carrying out to its logical conclusion the principles that had been taught me as a boy; and, as I am not going to seek your sympathy on false pretences, let me tell you I do not know today that there are any better principles﻿—there may be; I hope there are.
===Chinese===
「就这样，」他接着说，「二十一岁上，我发现自己骤然变穷；比大多数少年更习惯于认为，人生唯有为那些只属于富人的雅趣活着才值得；被教导不可把传统的道德律令视为理所当然；被教导凡事要追问每一行为的实际效用；然后落到西贡这样一个地方，混迹于我们都知道足以在那类地方代表欧洲文明的那一路人物中间；被派到那里谋生计；对周围人的一切趣味与享乐全然格格不入，同时却又轻而易举地发现：尽管我受过那般古怪的教养，论天生的男子气概，我在那里无人能及。其实，那地方正派的、有智识趣味的人只有一个，就是彼得斯；可是彼得斯——他不过比我大两三岁，而且我承认，我当时自以为他远不如我聪明——却把我置于他的羽翼之下，把纠正我当作他的使命，而这一套行不通。你们不难想象，在朗赫斯特登场之前的三年里，我怎样渐渐憎恶起那样的前程：在那班人中间庸庸碌碌、锱铢必较地捞钱，指望有朝一日攒下一小笔薄产告老还乡——到那时，我的肝和我的脑子也都毁了；倘若我安于那样的前程，你们也未必会更看得起我。无论如何，我没有安于。我打定主意，一有机会便摆脱那一切，而且我要的不止于此。到那地方不出三周，我就下定决心：要按一种规模赚钱，赚来的地位、社交圈子和消遣，须配得上我从小所受的训练。实际上我打定的主意是：在这世上为自己挣下那样一个位置——除了此刻室内的三个人和塔尔贝格外，人人都以为我已经挣到了手。至于实现这一决断的手段，我没有任何顾忌；在这一点上，我与周围人的分别只在于：我清楚自己没有顾忌，而且取而代之的，是一个既定的目标，我有足够的男子气概，肯为它追索一生。我也希望，如今目标既已落空，我仍有足够的男子气概，不至于太把性命当回事。倘若说我无所顾忌地追逐我的目的，那么我想，我不过是把幼年被灌输的那套原则推到了其逻辑的终点；我不打算靠假话骗取你们的同情，所以不妨告诉你们：直到今天，我也不知道世间还有没有更好的原则——也许有；我希望有。

===Original===
“I waited nearly three years, learning all I could about business and about the East, its trade and its resources, and waiting all the time for my opportunity which I knew would come, and which came. It came to me through Longhurst; but I must go back a little. I have said that Peters was my only equal in our society there. Now let me say, once for all, that in nothing that I am going to tell you do I wish to blame Peters more than I blame myself; but from the first we did not hit it off. Peters, as I have said, took on himself the part of my protector and adviser a little too obviously; he had not quite tact enough to do it well, and I was foolish enough in those days to resent what I thought his patronage. At first there was no harm done; Peters thought I should be the better if I entered more into such sport as there was in the place, for which I had very little taste, and he tried to make me do so by chaffing me about being a duffer, in his blunt way, which I thought rude, and that before other people. You would hardly imagine that I was ever shy, but I was; and, absurd as it seems, this added a good deal to my unhappiness in my new surroundings. I should very soon have got over that, for I soon found my way about the place, and my shyness quickly wore off; but worse than that followed. I was fond of arguing, and used to discuss all things in heaven and earth with Peters. You can easily suppose that his views and mine did not agree, and I daresay now that I pained him a good deal. I did not mean to do that, but I did mean to shock him sometimes, and so I often took a cynical line, by which I meant nothing at all, telling him the sharp things that I should do if I got the chance; and once or twice I was fool enough to pretend that all sorts of things of which Peters would not approve went on in our business. To my amazement I discovered after a time that Peters took all this nonsense seriously. I would have given anything to efface the impression that I had made, for though there are few men that I ever respected, Peters was one of them. But Peters became reserved towards me and impossible to get at. Then gossip came in between us. There is sometimes very spiteful gossip in a little European settlement in the East; and I am certain, though I cannot prove it, that a man there, with whom I had constant business, told Peters a story about a shady transaction which he said I was in. The transaction was real enough, but neither I nor my firm had any more to do with it than you. I know that this man told it to other people, for I have heard so from them, and I do not doubt that that was what finally turned Peters against me. I tried to tax Peters with having picked up this story, but he said something which sounded like disbelieving me, and I lost my temper and broke off; and from that day till we met again at Long Wilton we never exchanged any more words together, though we crossed one another’s path as you shall hear.
===Chinese===
「我等了将近三年，竭力学习商业，学习东方——它的贸易与资源——同时始终等着我深知必将来临的机会；机会果然来了。它是经朗赫斯特之手来的；但我得往回说一点。我说过，在我们那个小社会里，彼得斯是我唯一的对等之人。现在请让我一次说清：在接下来要告诉你们的一切里，我对彼得斯的责怪，绝不多于我对自己的责怪；但打从一开始，我们便处不来。如我所说，彼得斯把保护人兼顾问的角色担当得过于露骨；他行事不够圆通，难以做得妥帖，而我当年又愚蠢，把他的照拂当成了居高临下，心怀芥蒂。起初倒也无甚妨害；彼得斯觉得，那地方仅有的那点游猎消遣，我若多参与参与会有好处——我对这些本无多少兴致——他便试图用他那种直杠杠的打趣法子逗我加入，笑我是个笨蛋；这打趣在我看来失礼，而且是当着旁人的面。你们很难想象我竟也有羞怯的时候，可我确实羞怯过；而且尽管显得荒唐，这确也大大加深了我在新环境里的苦闷。这本不难过去，因为我不久便在那地方熟门熟路，羞怯也很快磨掉了；可更糟的事跟着来了。我喜好争辩，常与彼得斯纵论天地间万事。你们不难想到，他的见解与我的合不来；如今我敢说，我当时着实惹他痛心。我并非有意伤他，却的确有意偶尔惊一惊他，于是常常摆出一副愤世的腔调——那些话我自己本也全不当真——告诉他，若有机会，我会做出怎样的辣手之事；有一两次，我更是蠢到假装我们商行里正进行着种种彼得斯必定不齿的勾当。过了一阵我惊愕地发现，彼得斯竟把这些胡话当了真。只要能抹去我造成的印象，叫我付出什么代价都愿意，因为虽说这辈子我敬重过的人寥寥无几，彼得斯却是其中之一。可彼得斯对我变得矜持冷淡，再也无从接近。接着，流言又插进了我们中间。在东方的欧洲人小聚居地里，有时会有怨毒已极的流言；我虽无法证实，却确信当地一个与我常有业务往来的人，给彼得斯讲了一桩他说我牵涉其中的暧昧交易。那交易倒是实有其事，可我与我的商行同它的瓜葛，并不比你们二位多。我知道此人也把这故事讲给别人听过，因为有人这样告诉过我；我不怀疑，正是它最终使彼得斯对我翻了脸。我试着当面质问彼得斯是否听了这个故事，可他的回答听上去像是不信我，我便动了火气，就此决裂；从那天起，直到我们在朗威尔顿重逢，我们再没交谈过一句，尽管如你们将听到的那样，我们的人生屡屡交错。

===Original===
“Mind, again, I am not saying it was his fault; but it is in itself doing a young man a very ill turn to show him that you think him dishonest when as yet he is not, and it did me harm. Upon my soul, I was honest then; in fact, in that regard, most of my dealings throughout life would stand a pretty close scrutiny. But I have often thought that I might have become a much better man if Peters would have been my friend instead of suspecting me unjustly; and I confess that it rankles to this day, and all the more because I always respected Peters. After that, however, he did me some practical ill turns, disastrously ill turns; rightly enough, if he thought as he did. I must tell you that our separation came a very little while before Longhurst came to the place. Just afterwards I had an opening, a splendid opening; it would not have made me the rich man that I am, but it would have given me a good position right away, and what it would have saved me you shall judge. A very eminent person came to Saigon; he knew something of Peters and a little of me. He saw a great deal of Peters at Saigon, and he pressed him to accept a post that was in his gift in the Chinese Customs service. Peters refused. I suppose he was at that time thinking of coming home. The great man then spoke to me about it, and had all but offered it to me. How I should have jumped at it! But suddenly it all went off and he said no more to me. I believed that Peters warned him against me; possibly, being sore against Peters, I was mistaken; but at any rate that was what I ever afterwards believed. It was partly in desperate annoyance about this that I plunged into what then seemed my wild venture with Longhurst.
===Chinese===
「请注意，我再说一遍，我并不是说这是他的过错；可是，当一个年轻人尚未欺诈时，便让他看出你当他是欺诈之徒，这本身就是坑害一个年轻人不浅的事，而它确实伤了我。我敢以灵魂起誓，那时我是清白的；事实上，单就这一点而论，我一生的大多数交易都经得起相当严密的盘查。可我常常想，倘若当年彼得斯肯做我的朋友，而不是无端猜疑我，我或许会成为好得多的人；我承认，这根刺至今扎在心里，而且因为我向来敬重彼得斯，扎得愈发深。不过在那之后，他也实实在在坑了我几回，坑得灾难深重；倘若他果真那样想我，倒也无可指摘。我必须告诉你们，我们决裂后没过多久，朗赫斯特便到了那地方。紧接着，我遇上了一个机会，一个千载难逢的机会；它未必能让我成为今日这样的富豪，却能立时给我一个好位置，而它替我免掉了什么，你们自己判断吧。一位极显赫的人物来到西贡；他与彼得斯有几分交情，与我也略识。在西贡期间他常与彼得斯来往，力劝彼得斯接受他有权委任的一个中国海关的职位。彼得斯推辞了。我猜他那时正想着回英国。这位大人物随后便同我谈起此事，眼看就要把这职位许给我了。我巴不得立刻抓住它！可事情突然没了下文，他再没同我提起。我相信是彼得斯在他面前说了我的坏话；也许是我记恨彼得斯，看错了；但无论如何，此后我一直信以为真。我之所以一头扎进后来与朗赫斯特那桩看似发疯的冒险，一半正是出于对此事的怨愤难平。

===Original===
“And now I must tell you about Longhurst. He had been at some time, I suppose, a clever man; at least he had a wonderful store of practical knowledge about forests, mining and other matters, and he had travelled a great deal in all parts of that region of the world, and picked up many things which he wanted to turn to account. He had made a little money which he wished to increase, and he had a great scheme of organising and developing the trade of Southeastern Asia and its islands in various valuable kinds of timber, spices, gum, shellac, etc., etc. He promised anyone who could join him that in a few years, by exploiting certain yet undeveloped but most profitable sources of supply, he could get a monopoly of several important trades, the sago trade, for example. He set forth his scheme to the company generally at the English Club the first time I met him, and everybody laughed at him except me, who saw that if he got into the right hands there was something to be made out of his discoveries for him and other people. And as a matter of fact we did make something of them, more than I expected, but not what he expected. I did not make a large sum out of our joint venture, not much more than I could have made by staying where I was, but I got the knowledge of Eastern commerce, which has enabled me since to do what I have done.
===Chinese===
「现在我必须讲讲朗赫斯特其人了。我想他早年想必是个能干人；至少，他肚子里藏着一整套关于森林、采矿和其他行当的实用学问，那一带世界的四面八方他都跑遍了，捡来许多他指望派上用场的东西。他攒下一点钱，想把它变大，还怀着一个宏大的计划：把东南亚及其诸岛各色值钱的买卖——各种木材、香料、树胶、虫胶等等——组织起来，加以开拓。他对任何一个能与他合伙的人许诺：不出几年，只要开发几处尚未开垦却利润极厚的货源，他就能垄断好几门大宗买卖，西谷米贸易便是一例。我头一回见他，他就在英国俱乐部把他那套计划向众人兜售；人人都笑话他，只有我没有——我看出，他的那些发现若落到合适的人手里，于他于旁人都有钱可赚。事实上，我们也确实赚到了一些，比我的预期多，却不及他的预期。我在这桩合伙生意里没有赚到一大笔，并不比待在原地多挣多少，可我摸透了东方商业的门道，后来我之所以能成事，靠的正是这个。

===Original===
“I saw you smile just now, Mr. Callaghan, when I spoke of Longhurst getting into the right hands. Well he did; and I did not. He had been, as I said, a clever man, and there was something taking about him with his bluff, frank, burly air, but he was going off when I met him. People do go downhill if they spend all their lives in odd corners of the earth; and, though I did not know it at first, he had taken the surest road downhill, for he had begun to drink, and very soon it gained upon him like wildfire. When he once goes wrong no one can be so wrongheaded as a man like that, who thinks that he knows the world from having knocked about it a great deal doing nothing settled; and I should have found Longhurst difficult to deal with in any case. As it was, Longhurst dined with Peters the night before we left Saigon together. On the first day of our voyage he was very surly to me, and he said, ‘I heard something funny about you last night, Master Cartwright. I wish I had heard it before, that’s all.’ When I fired up and told him to say straight out what it was, he looked at me offensively, and went off into the smoking-room of the steamer to have another drink. That was not a cheerful beginning of our companionship, and I had my suspicion as to whom I ought to thank for it. I believe the same talebearer that I mentioned before had been telling Peters some yarn about my arrangements with Longhurst, which looked as if I was trying to swindle him, and that Peters had passed it on. I very soon found that Longhurst was not so simple as he seemed. I daresay he had meant honestly enough by me at first, but having got it into his thick head that I was a little too sharp, he made up his mind to be the sharper of the two; and the result was that if I was to be safe in dealing with him I must take care to keep the upper hand of him, and before long I made up my mind that my partner should go out of the firm. I could have made his fortune if he would have let me, but I meant that the concern should be mine and not his, and I did not disguise it from him. That was my great mistake. I do not know what story, if any, you have picked up about my dealings with Longhurst. He put about many stories when we had begun to quarrel﻿—for he had begun by that time, if not before, to drink freely﻿—but the matter that we finally quarrelled about was this. Of the various concessions which we started by obtaining (at least I started by obtaining them; that was to be my great contribution to the partnership), two only proved of very great importance﻿—one was from the Spanish Government of the Philippines and the other from the Government of Anam, and these, as it happened, were for three and four years, renewable under certain conditions but also revocable earlier in certain events. There was no trickery about that, though Longhurst may have thought there was. I simply could not get larger concessions with the means of persuasion (bribery, in other words) at our command. Subsequently I got renewals and extensions of these concessions to myself alone. To the best of my belief then and now the transaction held water in law and in equity, but whatever a lawyer might think of it, the common sense was this: Longhurst had become so reckless and so muddleheaded that nothing could any longer prosper under his control, if he had the control, and besides that, I never could have got the extended concessions at all if he was to be one of the concessionaires. There are some things which an Eastern Government or a Spanish Government cannot stand, and Longhurst’s treatment of the natives was one of them. But I must go back a bit. There were other things besides this which contributed to our quarrel. For one thing, odd as it may sound in speaking of two grown-up men, Longhurst bullied me﻿—physically bullied me. He was a very powerful man, more so, I should think, even than you, Mr. Callaghan, and when, as often happened, we were travelling alone together, he used to insist on my doing as he liked in small arrangements, by the positive threat of violence. To do him justice he did not do it when he was sober, and though in those days I was a weakly and timid man compared to what I have become, I soon learned how to stop it altogether. But you can easily imagine that I did not love him; and a bitter feeling towards his chief companion is not a wholesome thing for a man to carry about through a year or two of hard work in that climate (for it is a climate! none of the dry heat and bracing winters you have in Northern India); still I hope I did not bear him malice so much for that as for other things. I have said I have no scruples, but I have no liking for ruffianism and cruelty. I hate them for the same reason for which I hate some pictures and some architecture, because they are not to my taste. But I had, in out-of-the-way places, among weak savages, where law and order had not come, to put up with seeing deeds done which people here at home would not believe were done by their countrymen, and which a man who has served his days in an honourable service like the Indian Civil could believe in least of all. He had kicked a wretched man to death (for I have no doubt he died of it) the day he died himself.
===Chinese===
「方才我讲到朗赫斯特落进合适的人手里时，我看见你笑了，卡拉汉先生。哼，他确实是落进了，我却没有。如我所说，他曾经是个能干人，那副直爽、坦率、粗豪的气派也自有动人之处；可我结识他时，他已经在走下坡路了。人若把一辈子都耗在地球的偏僻角落里，是当真会走下坡路的；而且——虽然我起初并不知道——他走的那条下坡路最为稳妥：他已经沾上了酒，而且酒瘾像野火一样飞快地缠住了他。那样的人一旦走了邪路，谁也不及他那样死心眼：他一辈子游荡闲混、无所建树，却自以为深谙世事；所以无论如何，朗赫斯特于我都注定难缠。而事情又偏偏这样：我们一同离开西贡的前夜，朗赫斯特恰好在彼得斯那里用了晚饭。航行途中的头一天，他对我很是忿忿，说：『昨儿晚上我听说了你一桩有趣的事，卡特赖特少爷。可惜没早些听说，就这么回事。』我火了起来，叫他有话直说，他却无礼地瞪了我一眼，踱进轮船的吸烟室又去喝酒了。我们的同伴之谊就这样开了头，实在算不得愉快；该谢谁，我心里自有怀疑。我相信，又是先前提到过的那个搬弄是非的家伙，给彼得斯讲了一个关于我与朗赫斯特合股安排的故事，听着像是我存心诈骗他；而彼得斯又把它转述给了朗赫斯特。我不久便发现，朗赫斯特并非表面那样简单。我敢说，起初他对我的本意倒还诚实；可等他那颗木头脑袋认定我精明得过了头，他便打定主意要做两人里更精明的那一个；结果便是：我若要同他打交道而不吃亏，就必须设法始终压住他；不久我便拿定主意：我的合伙人必须从商行里出局。他若肯由着我来，我本可以让他也发一笔财；可我属意的是这份产业归我，不归他，而这一点我不对他掩饰。这便是我的大错。我不知道你们可曾听到什么关于我与朗赫斯特交易的说法——如果有的话。我们开始失和之后，他到处散布了许多说法——因为到那时他若非更早，已经放开量喝酒了——但我们最终闹翻的事由是这一件。我们起步时弄到手的各色特许权（至少是我弄到手的；那本该是我在合伙中最大的贡献），后来证明真正要紧的只有两项：一项出自菲律宾的西班牙政府，一项出自安南（Anam）政府；不巧，这两项的期限分别为三年与四年，在一定条件下可以续展，遇有某些事由却也可提前废止。这里面没有半点欺诈，尽管朗赫斯特也许以为有。凭我们当时使得上的说服手段（说白了就是贿赂），我根本拿不到更长期的特许权。后来，这两项特许权的续展与扩充，我办到了自己一个人的名下。无论当时还是现在，我都深信这笔交易在法律上、在衡平上站得住脚；但不论律师会作何想，常识是：朗赫斯特已经变得那样鲁莽、那样昏聩，任何事业只要归他掌管，便再无兴旺之望——倘若归他掌管的话；何况，只要他还是特许权人之一，那扩大的特许权我根本一分也拿不到。有些事情，是东方政府也好、西班牙政府也好，断然容忍不了的；朗赫斯特对待土人的那套手段，便是其一。不过我得略往回说。除了这件事，还有别的事促成了我们的失和。有一件，说来在两个成年男人之间听着古怪：朗赫斯特凌虐我——凭气力凌虐我。他是个力气极大的人，我想比你还大，卡拉汉先生；而每逢我们——这是常有的事——单独结伴旅行，连些鸡毛蒜皮的安排他也要逼我依他，凭的就是明晃晃的动粗威胁。平心而论，他清醒时不来这一套；而且那时的我比起今日的我，还是个孱弱胆小的人，可我不久便学会了如何叫它彻底断根。但你们不难想见，我爱不起他来；在那个气候里（那可真叫气候！全没有你们北印度那种干燥的热、爽利的冬天）苦干上一两年，怀里揣着对头号同伴的一腔怨毒，可不是滋养人的东西；不过我希望，我记恨他，与其说是为此，不如说是为别的。我说过我无所顾忌；可我对横暴与残忍没有好感。我恨它们，同我恨某些画、恨某些建筑是出于同一个缘故：因为不合我的趣味。可是在那些穷乡僻壤，在弱小的蛮族中间，在法律与秩序尚未照到的地方，我不得不眼看一桩桩勾当做出来——国内的人们绝不会相信那是他们的同胞所为，而在印度文职那样体面的部门里当满过差的人，更是最难以相信。就在他自己毙命的那天，他把一个可怜虫活活踢死了（我毫不怀疑那人正是死于这一顿踢）。

===Original===
“But why do I make all these excuses? for, after all, what did I do that needs so much excuse? I told Longhurst plainly what I had done about the concessions and what I proposed to do for him, and he seemed to fall in with it all, and then he went home for a month’s holiday in England. I suppose he saw some lawyer, probably Thalberg, and got it into his head that he could make out a case of fraud against me. At any rate, when he returned, he seemed surly; he did not have it out with me straight, but he began to make extravagant demands of me and threaten me vaguely with some exposure if I did not give in to them, which of course I did not. Then he quarrelled about it in his cups, for the cups were getting more and more frequent, and several times over he got so violent as to put me in actual fear of my life. And at last, unhappily for him, it came to a real encounter. We had visited the island of Sulu, where I had reason to think we might establish a branch of our business, and after two or three days in an inland town we were returning to the coast, expecting to be picked up by a Chinese junk which was to take us back. The evening before we started down he produced a packet of documents and brandished it at me as if it contained something very damaging to me, and I could see plainly (for I have an eye for handwriting) that on the top of it was an envelope addressed by Peters. I am not justified in inferring from this that Peters﻿—who had seen Longhurst several times since he had seen me﻿—had again been repeating to him some malicious falsehood with which he had been stuffed before he left Saigon; but can you wonder that I did infer it? On the march down﻿—when we were alone, for we had sent on our servants before﻿—Longhurst began again more savagely than ever, and for about an hour he heaped all sorts of charges and vile insinuations upon me, which I answered for a while as patiently as I could. At last, breaking off in the middle of a curse, he fell into silence. He strode on angrily ahead for a hundred yards or so. Then at a rocky part of the path, where I was below him, he turned suddenly. He hurled at me a great stone which narrowly missed me, and then he came rushing and clambering back down the path at me. I fired (he turned as I fired). That was the end. Was it murder?” He paused and then braced himself up as he answered his own question. “Yes, it was, because I was angry, not afraid, and because I could easily have run away, only for some reason I did not mean to.
===Chinese===
「可我何必作这许多辩解？说到底，我做了什么需要如此开脱的事呢？我把特许权上的所作所为、以及我打算给他的安排，原原本本告诉了朗赫斯特；他看上去对一切全盘应承，随后便回英国休了一个月的假。我猜他去见了某个律师，多半就是塔尔贝格，并且认定他能给我安一个欺诈的罪名。无论如何，他回来之后便阴沉着脸；他不肯同我当面摊牌，却开始对我提出种种漫天要价，又含含糊糊地拿曝光来威胁我，逼我让步——我自然没有让步。后来他便乘着酒劲为此吵闹——因为酒杯越来越勤了——有几回他凶性大发，当真叫我担忧起自己的性命。最终，不幸是对他而言，真的动上了手。我们去苏禄岛跑了一趟——我有理由认为，我们可以在那里设一个分号；在岛内一个市镇盘桓了两三天后，我们正返回海岸，等着一条来接我们回去的中国帆船。动身下山的前夜，他掏出一包文件，冲我挥舞，仿佛里面装着于我大为不利的东西；我看得分明（我对笔迹素有眼力），最上面是一只彼得斯亲笔书写地址的信封。据此便断言彼得斯——自见过我之后，他又见过朗赫斯特几面——再次向他复述了那些恶毒的谗言（那些话是他离开西贡前被人灌进耳朵的），我并无确凿的凭据；可你们能怪我偏这样断言吗？下山的路上——我们已打发仆人先行，只剩两人——朗赫斯特又开了腔，比以往更加凶横；约莫一个钟头里，他把种种罪名和污秽的暗讽劈头盖脸砸到我身上，我隐忍着应付了一阵。终于，一句咒骂骂到一半，他骤然住口，陷入沉默。他怒冲冲抢到前头，大步走出一百来码。随后，在路径多石的一段——我所站的位置在他下方——他猛然回身，朝我掷来一块巨石，擦身而过，险些砸中；随即他便顺着路径连冲带爬地朝我扑来。我开了枪（开枪时他正转身）。就这样完了。这算谋杀吗？」他顿了顿，随即挺了挺身子，自己回答了自己的问题。「算。因为我是出于愤怒，不是出于恐惧；也因为本可以轻易逃开，只是出于某种缘故，我不打算逃。

===Original===
“But I am foolish to weary you with all this long preliminary story, for, after all, what do you care about Longhurst; it is Peters, your own friend, about whom you care. You think that he came to suspect me of murdering Longhurst, and I killed him for that; but as sure as I killed him, that was not﻿—that was *not* what made me do it.”
===Chinese===
「可我真傻，竟拿这么长一段开场白熬磨你们；说到底，朗赫斯特与你们何干？你们在意的是彼得斯，是你们自己的朋友。你们以为，是他起意怀疑我谋害了朗赫斯特，我才因此杀他；可是正如确确实实是我杀了他一样确凿——不是，那*不是*促使我下手的原因。」

===Original===
Vane-Cartwright sat for a long time with his face covered with his hands. At last he sat up and looked me straight in the face. “Mr. Driver, did you never suspect there was a romance in Peters’ life of which you knew nothing? I did know of it, and I honoured him for it, but I hated him for it too. Certainly you did not suspect that there was a romance in mine. It does not seem likely that a great passion should come to a calculating man like me, with the principles of conduct of which I have made no secret today. But such things do happen, and a great passion came late in life to me. And here is the cruel thing, which almost breaks my philosophy down, and makes me think that after all there is a curse upon crime. It ought to have enriched and ennobled my life, ought it not? It came at just the moment, in just the shape, and with all the attendant accidents to ruin me.
===Chinese===
韦恩-卡特赖特双手掩面，坐了许久。终于，他直起身子，直直地望着我的脸。「德赖弗先生，你可曾疑心过，彼得斯的一生里有一段你全然不知的情史？我却是知道的；我为此敬重他，也为此恨他。当然，你们绝没有疑心过我的一生里也有一段情史。像我这样一个工于算计的人，又抱着今天毫不隐讳的那套行事准则，一场伟大的热恋似乎不该落到我头上。可这种事偏会发生；一场伟大的热恋，在我人生的晚季来了。而残酷正在这里——残酷得几乎摧垮我的哲学，使我不禁想到：犯罪到底还是背着诅咒的。它本该丰盈我、升华我的生命，难道不是吗？可它来的时机、来的模样，连同随之而来的一切变故，偏偏样样都是来毁掉我的。

===Original===
“It began five years ago. Miss Denison and her parents were staying at Pau. I was in the same hotel and I met them. I knew nothing then of their position and wealth and all that, for I had not been long in London. I loved her, and a great hope came into my life. One begins to weary after a while of toiling just to make money for oneself. For a few days all seemed changed, the whole world was new and bright to me. Suddenly I got an intimation from the father of the lady that my calls were no longer acceptable. I could not imagine the reason. I asked for an interview to explain matters, and he refused it. I left at once. I did not yet know how hard I should find it to give her up. It was only as I left the hotel that I learned that Peters, Peters whom I had not met since we quarrelled at Saigon, and of whom I last heard of the day that Longhurst died, was in the hotel and had called on my friends. Now I see clearly that I am wrong to draw inferences, but again, I ask, could I help inferring what I did?
===Chinese===
「那是五年前的事了。丹尼森小姐和她的父母正在波城（Pau）小住。我住在同一家旅馆，认识了他们。那时我对他们的地位、家财等等一概不知，因为我到伦敦不久。我爱上了她，一个巨大的希望进入了我的生命。只为替自己赚钱而操劳，日子久了，人是会厌倦的。有那么几天，一切仿佛都变了，整个世界于我焕然一新、光彩夺目。忽然，那位小姐的父亲知会我：我的拜访不再受欢迎。我猜不出缘由。我求见一面，以便解释，他拒绝了。我当即离开。那时我还不知道，放弃她于我会何等艰难。也正是在离开旅馆之际，我才得知彼得斯——那个自西贡决裂后再未谋面、而我在朗赫斯特死去的当天最后听到他消息的彼得斯——正住在那家旅馆里，还拜访过我的朋友们。如今我看得分明，我作推断是不对的；可我还是要问：当年教我如何能不作那样的推断？」

===Original===
“More than four years passed. I tried hard to create new interests for myself in artistic things, making all sorts of collections; and I developed an ambition to be a personage in London society. Then I saw Miss Denison again, and I knew that I had not forgotten her, and could not do so. I knew now what had happened, and so I absolutely insisted on an explanation. I had it out with the father. I satisfied him absolutely. In a few weeks’ time I was engaged. For the first time in my life I was happy. That was only a month before I came to Long Wilton. I must tell you that Peters had known the Denisons long, and that I knew Miss Denison had been fond of him, but we naturally did not talk of him much, and I did not know he was at Long Wilton. There, to my complete surprise, I saw Peters again. I would not avoid him, but I certainly did not wish to meet him. He, however, came up to me and spoke quite cordially. I do not know whether he had reflected and thought he had been hard on me, but he seemed to wish to make amends, and I at that time, just for a few short hours, had not got it in my heart to be other than friendly with any man.
===Chinese===
「四年多过去了。我竭力在艺术上为自己营造新的趣味，收藏各色物件；又渐渐生出一股抱负，要在伦敦社交界做一个人物。后来我再次见到了丹尼森小姐，我知道自己没有忘记她，也忘不掉她。这时我已经知道当年发生了什么，于是坚决要求一个解释。我同她的父亲当面把话说清。我让他彻底信了服。不出几个星期，我们订了婚。平生第一次，我幸福了。那不过是我在来朗威尔顿前一个月的事。我必须告诉你们：彼得斯与丹尼森一家相识已久；我也知道丹尼森小姐曾经钟情于他；但我们自然不常谈起他，我更不知道他就在朗威尔顿。在那里，我全然不曾料到地，又见到了彼得斯。我不愿躲避他，却也实在不想同他相见。然而他主动走上前来，言辞极为恳切。我不知他是否经过反思，觉得自己从前待我太苛；但他似乎有心修好；而我那时候——恰恰在那短短几个钟头里——心里对任何人都硬不起半点敌意。

===Original===
“That evening I spent at his house. You, Mr. Callaghan, were there, and you must have seen that something happened. I at any rate saw that something I said had revived all Peters’ suspicions of me, and this time with the addition of a suspicion, which was true, that I had murdered Longhurst.

“Now, I ask you, if you have any lingering idea that that was why I killed him, how was it possible that he could ever prove me guilty? Have you any inkling of how he could have done it? I have not. Now what could induce me, on account of a mere idle suspicion on the part of a man who need be nothing to me, to run the risk amounting almost to certainty of being hanged for murdering him?
===Chinese===
「那天晚上，我便是从他家里度过的。卡拉汉先生，你也在场，你必定看出出了事。至少我看出来了：我的某句话唤醒了彼得斯对我全部的旧疑，而且这一回还添上了一条新的疑心——这一条倒是真的——疑心我谋害了朗赫斯特。

「那么我问你们，倘若你们还残存着一丝念头，以为我杀他是为此，他又怎能证明我有罪呢？你们可有一星半点的眉目，知道他如何证明得了？我是没有。再说，一个于我本可毫无干系的人，仅凭他一点捕风捉影的疑心，能拿什么诱使我甘冒杀害他的风险——冒那几乎十拿九稳要上绞架的险？」

===Original===
“But my conscience was active then, for a reason which any man who has loved may guess. I wanted to clear up all with Peters. I could not get him alone that evening, and I had to go next day. I returned the first day I could, bringing certain materials for clearing up the early transaction about which he had first suspected me. I was honestly determined to make a clean breast to him about Longhurst. You can hardly wonder that I meant to feel my way with him in this. I tried to get to close quarters with him. Mr. Callaghan saw enough to know how unsuccessful I was. I tried all the time, again and again, to draw Peters into intimate talk about our days in the East, but he always seemed to push me away. I determined very soon to obtain a letter from a friend, whom I will not name now, who knew how Longhurst had treated me, which I could show to Peters; so I wrote to him. But in the meantime relations with Peters grew harder and harder. I will not spin out excuses, but all his old animosity to me returned, and I began while I was waiting for that letter to feel once again the old rancour I had felt. This man had hurt me by suspecting me falsely, when, had he shown me confidence, he could have made a better man of me; he had spoilt my best chance of a career; he had poisoned my relations with Longhurst, and so brought about the very crime of which he was now lying in wait to accuse me; he had thwarted my love for four miserable years. On the top of all that came this letter” (he had held a letter in his hand all the time he was speaking), “and it shall speak for itself. But first one question. You may remember when you first saw me at Long Wilton. Well, I came really as it happened upon an errand for Miss Denison. Mrs. Nicholas, in the village, you may not know, had been her nurse. But that does not matter. Between my first visit and my return, do you happen to remember that a Mrs. Bulteel was staying at the hotel, and visited Mr. Peters of whom she was an old friend?”
===Chinese===
「可我那时的良心是醒着的——缘由何在，凡爱过的人都猜得到。我想同彼得斯把一切说清。那天晚上我寻不到单独同他谈话的机会，次日又不得不离去。我便在最早可能的一天赶了回来，带来若干材料，足以澄清他最初起疑的那桩早期交易。我真心实意打定主意，要向他和盘托出朗赫斯特的事。这件事上我想先试探他的口风，你们不至于奇怪。我设法凑近他。卡拉汉先生所见，足知我何等徒劳。我一而再、再而三地想把彼得斯引到谈及我们东方岁月的知心话里去，他却总像是把我推开。我不久便决意去弄一封来信——写信的是一位朋友，他深知朗赫斯特怎样待我，此人我现在不愿说出名字——好拿给彼得斯看；于是我给他去了信。可与此同时，我与彼得斯的关系越来越僵。我不想絮絮辩解，总之他对我的旧怨全都回来了；而在等信的那段日子里，我也重新尝到了当年的积恨。这个人，曾以无端的猜疑伤过我——而倘若他肯信任我，本可把我造就成一个好得多的人；他毁了我前程中最好的一次机遇；他毒化了我与朗赫斯特的关系，从而一手促成了他此刻正张网以待要指控我的那桩罪案；他还断送我的爱情，害我凄凄惶惶苦了四年。偏偏在这一切之上，这封信来了」——他讲话时手里一直捏着一封信——「让它自己说话吧。但先有一问。你们或许记得初次在朗威尔顿见到我的情形。是这样：我那次来，其实是碰巧替丹尼森小姐办事。村里的尼古拉斯太太（Mrs. Nicholas）——你们未必知道——做过她的乳母。不过这无关紧要。在我初来与我再来之间，你们可碰巧记得，有位布尔蒂尔太太（Mrs. Bulteel）正住在旅馆，还拜访过彼得斯先生？她是他的老友。

===Original===
Callaghan remembered that it was so.

“Mrs. Bulteel is, I have always supposed, the lady referred to in this letter, which reached me (will you note?) by the five o’clock post at Peters’ house, seven hours before I killed him.”
===Chinese===
卡拉汉记得确有其事。

「我一向认定，信里提到的那位女士就是布尔蒂尔太太。这封信——请你们注意——是五点钟的邮班送到彼得斯宅里的，在我杀他之前七个小时。」

===Original===
He passed the letter to me without looking at me. Callaghan and I read it together. It was in a lady’s hand, signed with the name of Lady Denison, the young lady’s mother. It appeared to be written in great agitation. Its purport was that the young lady had resolved, so her mother found, to break off her engagement with Vane-Cartwright. She had formerly loved another man, whose name the mother thought she must not mention, though probably Vane-Cartwright knew it, but had supposed that he did not care for her or had given up doing so. She had now learned from an officious lady friend, who had lately seen this old lover, that he cared for her still; that he had concealed his passion when he found she favoured Vane-Cartwright, but that having now apparently quarrelled with Vane-Cartwright he had authorised her to let this be known if she saw her opportunity. The mother concluded by saying that she had so far failed in reasoning with her daughter, who had wished to write and break off her engagement, and all she could do was to lay on her the absolute command not to write to Vane-Cartwright at all for the present.
===Chinese===
他不看我，把信递给了我。我和卡拉汉一起读。信出于女子手笔，署名是那位年轻小姐的母亲——丹尼森夫人（Lady Denison）。信似是在极大的激动中写就的。大意是：做母亲的发现，女儿已经决意解除与韦恩-卡特赖特的婚约。女儿从前爱过另一个男人；那人的名字，母亲认为不便说出，虽然韦恩-卡特赖特多半已经知道；但母亲一直以为，那人并不把她放在心上，或者早已不放在心上。如今她从一位热心过头的女友——这位女友新近见过那位旧情人——那里得知：他依然恋着她；当初发觉她属意韦恩-卡特赖特时，他曾把这段情压下不露；而如今他显然与韦恩-卡特赖特闹翻了，便授权这位女友，若见时机合适，不妨把此事挑明。信末，母亲写道：她劝谕女儿至今无果；女儿本想亲笔写信解除婚约，她所能做的，只有严令女儿眼下绝不要给韦恩-卡特赖特去信。

===Original===
“There is only one comment to make on that letter,” said Vane-Cartwright. “You may wonder why I should have assumed that it was hopeless. Well, I knew the lady better than you, better than her mother did, and knew that if her old attachment had returned it had returned to stay. Besides, I read this letter with my rival sitting in the room (you two gentlemen were sitting in the room too as it happens), and when hard, self-contained people do come under these influences, they do not give way to them by halves.

“Thank you,” said Vane-Cartwright, when we had read and returned the letter. “I am glad you have heard me so patiently. That all this makes me less of a villain than you thought me, I do not pretend to say; but I think you will understand why I wished some men whom I respected, as I respect you, to know my story. I do not suggest for a moment that it should influence your present action. Here I am, as I said to begin with, your prisoner. Of course you see that society is just as safe from future murders from me as from any man. But if your principles of justice demand life for life, or if human feeling makes you resolve to avenge your friend, that is just what I came here expecting. I am the last man in the world who could give an unprejudiced opinion on the ethics of punishment.”

He ended with a quiet and by no means disagreeable smile.
===Chinese===
「关于这封信，只有一句可说，」韦恩-卡特赖特道。「你们或许奇怪，我凭什么认定大局已无可挽回。是这样的：那位小姐，我比你们了解，比她母亲还了解；我知道，旧情若真回来了，便是回来长住不走的。何况，我读这封信的时候，我的情敌就坐在同一间屋里（碰巧，二位先生也坐在屋里）；而冷硬自持的人，一旦真被这样的情感俘获，是从不肯半心半意屈从的。」

我们读完交还了信。「多谢，」韦恩-卡特赖特说，「二位听得这样耐心，我很欣慰。我并不妄称，这一切使我比你们心目中的恶棍好了几分；但我想你们能明白：为什么我希望由几个我敬重的人——正如我敬重二位——来听我的故事。我一刻也没有暗示，它应当左右你们眼下的行动。我人在此处，正如开头所说，是二位的俘虏。你们当然看得出：论日后行凶的可能，社会防我一人，同防任何一个旁人一样稳妥。但倘若你们的正义信条要求命抵一命，或者人之常情使你们决意为朋友复仇——那正是我来时便料到的。天底下再没有比我更没资格对惩罚的伦理作不偏不倚之论的人了。」

他说完，静静一笑，那笑容绝无不快之意。

===Original===
As I have often said I make no sort of pretence to report any talk quite correctly, and here, where the manner of the talk is of special importance, I feel more than ever my incompetence to report it. I can only say that the singular confession, of which I have striven to repeat the purport, was in reality delivered with a great deal of restrained eloquence, and with occasional most moving play of facial expression, all the more striking in a man whom I had seldom before seen to move a muscle of his face unnecessarily. It was delivered to two men of whom one (myself) was physically overwrought, while the other (Callaghan), naturally emotional, was at the commencement in the fullest elation of triumphant pursuit, in other words, ready to recoil violently.

We sat, I do not know how long, each waiting for the other to speak. Vane-Cartwright sat meanwhile neither looking at us nor moving his countenance﻿—only the fingers of one hand kept drumming gently upon his knee.

At last I did what I think I never did but once before, obeyed an impulse almost physical, to speak words which my mouth seemed to utter mechanically. If they were the words of reason, they were not the words of my conscious thought, for that was busy with all, and more than all the scruples which had ever made this business hard to me.
===Chinese===
正如我一再说过的，我从不假装能一字不差地转述任何谈话；而此地，谈话的方式尤其要紧，我便更觉无力转述。我只能说，这篇我已竭力转述其大意的独特自白，实际讲述时带着大量克制之下的雄辩，间或又有极为动人的面部神情变化；发生在一个我素来罕见他无故牵动一根面部肌肉的人身上，愈发惊人。这番话是对两个人说的：一个（我自己）身心俱已绷至极限；另一个（卡拉汉）天生易动感情，起初正处在追凶得胜的最大亢奋之中——换句话说，正处在猛然反弹的边缘。

我们坐着，不知坐了多久，各自等对方开口。韦恩-卡特赖特坐在那里，既不看我们，脸上也纹丝不动——只有一只手的指头，在他膝上轻轻叩个不停。

终于，我做了一件我想平生只做过一次的事：服从了一股近乎生理性的冲动，说出一席我的嘴仿佛在机械吐出的话。这些话若算是理智之言，却不是我清醒头脑里的所思所想，因为我的头脑彼时正被重重顾虑占满——这桩案子从头到尾折磨我的那些顾虑，以及更多的顾虑。

===Original===
“Mr. Vane-Cartwright,” I said, “it is my painful duty to tell you at once that I do not believe one word you have said, except what I knew already.”

He went white for a moment; then quickly recomposed himself and inclined his head slightly with a politely disdainful expression.
===Chinese===
「韦恩-卡特赖特先生，」我说，「我必须忍痛立刻奉告：你方才所说的话，除我已知道的那部分外，我一个字也不信。」

他霎时白了脸色；随即迅速恢复镇定，微微颔首，带着一种彬彬有礼的轻蔑神情。

===Original===
“Oh, Driver,” said Callaghan, in a gentle tone, and he arose and paced the room. He was strangely moved. To begin with, though he had felt nothing but remorseless glee in his share in hunting his victim down, he would in any case have felt great repugnance at giving him the coup de grâce. But then he had once taken the step of inviting that victim into his own room; he had sat there for an hour and a half with that victim by his own fireside, telling his life-story and implicitly pleading for his life. And the pleading had been conducted under the flattering pretext that it was not pleading at all but the instinctive confidence of a redoubtable antagonist, in one whom he respected for having beaten him. As for the story itself, Callaghan did not exactly believe it; on the contrary, I found afterwards that, while I had not got beyond a vague sense that the whole story was a tissue of lies, he had noted with rapid acuteness each of the numerous points of improbability in it; but to his mind (Irish, if I may say publicly what I have said to him) the fact that the story appealed to his imaginative sympathy was almost as good as its being true, and what in respect of credibility was wanting to its effect was quite made good by Callaghan’s admiration for the intrepidity with which the man had carried out this attempt on us. And the story did appeal to his sympathy, he had sympathised with his early struggles, he had sympathised still more with the suggestion of passion in his final crime, and (Irish again) had ignored the fact that on the criminal’s own showing the crime conceived in passion had been carried through with a cold-blooded meanness of which Callaghan’s own nature had no trace. Lastly, he was genuinely puzzled by the problem as to the morality of vengeance which Vane-Cartwright had raised with so dexterously slight a touch.
===Chinese===
「唉，德赖弗，」卡拉汉轻声说道，起身在屋里踱起步来。他激动得异乎寻常。首先，尽管他在把猎物追到绝路的份内之事里只感到过无情的快意，可无论如何，亲手补上致命一击，总叫他万分不愿。何况，他还迈出过那样一步：把这猎物请进了自己的房间；同这猎物在自家炉边对坐了一个半钟头，听他讲述生平，实则听他隐含地为自己求一条生路。而这求饶又是打着一套悦人的幌子进行的：那根本不是求饶，而是一个可畏的对手对战胜了自己的人油然而生的信任——那人正因战胜过他而为他所敬重。至于那故事本身，卡拉汉谈不上相信；恰恰相反，我后来才知道，当我还只停留在一种模糊的感觉、觉得整个故事是一派谎言织成的罗网时，他早已以敏捷的锐眼逐一记下了其中大量的破绽；但在他心里（爱尔兰人的心，请容我当众说出我对他说过的话），一个故事能唤起他想象的同情，几乎就等于它是真的；至于可信度上的欠缺，卡拉汉对这人向我们施行此计的那份胆魄的钦佩，早已绰绰有余地补足了。而这故事也确实唤起了他的同情：他同情过此人的早年挣扎，更同情他最后一桩罪行里透出的激情，并且（又是爱尔兰人）无视了这一事实：照罪犯自己的供认，那桩起于激情的罪行，是怀着一种冷血的卑劣干到底的，而卡拉汉自己的天性里没有半分这种东西。最后，韦恩-卡特赖特用那样轻巧的一触所提出的复仇伦理问题，也着实叫他困惑。

===Original===
Whatever his motive, Callaghan was upon the point of resolving that, at least from his own room, where the criminal had come to appeal to his mercy, that criminal should go away free. And if Callaghan had so resolved I should have been powerless for a time; he was prepared and I was not as to the steps immediately to be taken to secure Vane-Cartwright’s arrest. But it seems, if for once I may use that phrase with so little or else so deep a meaning, that the luck had departed from Vane-Cartwright. At this crisis of his fate a device of his own recoiled upon him with terrible force.

“I cannot do it! I cannot do it!” Callaghan was exclaiming, when the door opened and a telegram was brought for me. This was the message: “Clarissa terribly ill, symptoms poison, Bancroft, Fidele.” It meant that my wife was dying at the friend’s villa to which she had gone, and dying by that man’s means, and it was certified by the use of the password which my wife had told me to expect. I did not reflect and I did not speak; I grasped Callaghan’s arm and I put the telegram in his hand. He knew enough to understand the message well. He read it with an altered face. He passed it to Vane-Cartwright and said: “Read that, and take it for my answer.” I should doubt if Vane-Cartwright had often been violently angry, but he was now. He dashed the telegram down with a curse. “The fool,” he said, and he gasped with passion, “if he was going to try that trick, why did not he do it before?” Callaghan stepped up to me, put his big arms round me, and for a moment hugged me in them, with tears in his eyes. Then without a word he strode across the room, and, before I could see what was happening, Vane-Cartwright’s hands were tied behind his back with a great silk handkerchief.
===Chinese===
不管他的动机是什么，卡拉汉眼看就要拿定这样的主意：至少从他自己的房间里——罪犯正是到这里来恳求他的慈悲的——这罪犯应当全身而退。倘若卡拉汉真这样拿定主意，我便一时无能为力；至于确保逮捕韦恩-卡特赖特须立即采取的步骤，他是有所准备的，我却两手空空。可是——容我破例用一用这个含义或浅或深的说法——运气似乎已经弃韦恩-卡特赖特而去了。在他命运的这个关口，他自己设下的一记机关，以可怕的力量反弹到了他自己身上。

「我下不了手！我下不了手！」卡拉汉正这样喊着，门开了，有人送来一封给我的电报。电文是：「克拉丽莎（Clarissa）重病垂危，症状为中毒。班克罗夫特（Bancroft）。Fidele」这意思是：我的妻子正在她去做客的那位朋友的别墅里垂危，而且正是死在那人的手段之下；电文用了妻子叮嘱我留心防范的暗语，足证不假。我没有思索，也没有说话；我一把抓住卡拉汉的胳膊，把电报塞进他手里。他知道的内情，足以让他彻底明白这封电报的分量。他读着电报，脸色都变了。他把电报递给韦恩-卡特赖特，说：「拿去看，这就是我的答复。」我想，韦恩-卡特赖特此人怕是难得狂怒，可这一回他狂怒了。他咒骂一声，把电报狠狠摔了下去。「那蠢货，」他说，怒气逼得他直喘，「既然要使这一手，他先前为什么不用？」卡拉汉抢步到我面前，张开他那双大胳膊把我搂住，搂了片刻，眼里噙着泪。随后他一言不发，大步横穿房间；还没等我看清是怎么回事，韦恩-卡特赖特的双手已被一条大绸手帕反剪着绑在了背后。

## XXII

===Original===
My story draws towards its close, and of mystery or of sudden peril it has little more to tell. Upon one point, the most vital to me, let me not give the reader a moment’s suspense. My wife did not die of poison, had not been poisoned, had not been ill, had not sent that telegram. What had happened was this: on one single occasion she had not despatched her own message herself; through the misunderstanding or too prompt courtesy of her host’s butler, the telegram which she had written had been taken by a messenger, and it had fallen into the hands of the enemy’s watchful emissary. It had revealed to him the password which my wife used to me; and in its place there had gone over the wires a message which would indeed have called me back at any stage of the pursuit, but which was fated to arrive neither sooner nor later than the moment when it must destroy Vane-Cartwright’s last hope of escape.

I say not later, for indeed I have evidence strong enough for my now suspicious mind that Vane-Cartwright had endeavoured to prepare his escape in the event of his failure to persuade Callaghan and myself. An unoccupied flat immediately below Callaghan’s had the day before been engaged by a nameless man, who paid a quarter’s rent in advance, and on the day of his interview with us, several strange persons, who were never seen there again, arrived with every sign of belated haste; but, whatever accident had delayed them, they arrived a quarter of an hour after we had left.

And so on the 15th of May, 1897, nearly sixteen months after Peters’ death, his murderer was handed over to the police, with information which, including as it did the fact of his confession, ensured their taking him into custody.
===Chinese===
我的故事已近尾声，关于谜团与突如其来的凶险，已没有多少可讲的了。唯有一点——于我至为紧要的一点——我不愿让读者悬念片刻。我的妻子没有死于毒药，没有中过毒，没有害过病，也没有发出过那封电报。事情的原委是这样的：唯独有那么一次，她没有亲自去发自己的电报；由于主人家管家的误会——或是他过于殷勤——她写好的电文交由信差代发，竟落入了敌方那警觉的密使手中。电文向他泄露了妻子与我之间使用的暗语；取而代之从电线上发来的，是另一封电报——那封电报固然能在追缉的任何一个关口把我召回，却偏偏注定不早不晚，恰好在必然摧毁韦恩-卡特赖特最后一丝脱逃希望的那一刻送到。

我之所以说不晚，是因为在我如今这颗惯于疑虑的心看来，确有足够分量的凭据，表明韦恩-卡特赖特早已设法备下脱身之计，以防说服我和卡拉汉不成。就在与我们会面的前一天，紧挨卡拉汉楼下那套空置的公寓，被一个不留姓名的人租了下来，还预付了一季的租金；到了他与我们面谈的那天，又有几个来历陌生、此后再无人见过的人，带着一副迟到误事的仓皇赶到；然而无论是什么意外耽搁了他们，他们抵达时，我们已离开一刻钟了。

就这样，1897年5月15日，在彼得斯死后将近十六个月，杀害他的凶手被移交警方，随案移交的种种信息——其中便包括他供认不讳这一事实——确保警方将他收押在案。

===Original===
Then I, in my turn, became Callaghan’s prisoner. I arrived at Charing Cross station in good time for the night train, and found my luggage already there and registered, and my ticket taken. Our tickets taken, rather, for, protest as I might, I was escorted by Callaghan, indeed nursed (and I needed it) the whole way to Florence, and to the villa where my wife was staying. One item remains untold to complete for the present the account of the debt which I owe him. We had hardly left Charing Cross when his quick wits arrived at precisely that explanation of the telegram which in happy fact was true; but all the way, talkative man though he was, he refrained from vexing my bruised mind with a hope which, he knew, I should not be able to trust.

When he had learnt at the door that his happy foreboding was true, no entreaty would induce him to stay and break bread. He returned at once to England, leaving me to enter alone to that reunion of which I need say nothing, nor even tell how much two people had hungered for it.
===Chinese===
随后，轮到我做了卡拉汉的囚徒。我提前赶到查令十字（Charing Cross）车站去搭夜车，却发现我的行李早已送到、托运停当，车票也买好了——不如说，我们的车票买好了；因为任凭我怎么抗议，一路都是卡拉汉押送着我，确切说是护持着我（我也确实需要护持），直到佛罗伦萨，直到我妻子客居的那座别墅。要把目前我所欠他的这笔情分交代完整，还有一桩事没有说出。我们刚离开查令十字，他那敏捷的头脑便已恰好推想到那封电报的来由——而可喜的是，事实正是如此；可一路上，他虽素来健谈，却始终克制着，不肯拿一个希望来撩拨我受伤的心，因为他知道，那希望我是万万不敢相信的。

在别墅门口，他一得知那桩可喜的预感应验了，便任凭什么恳求也留他不住、吃不成一口饭。他当即折返英格兰，留我独自进去赴那场团圆——那团圆，我无须多说，甚至两个人曾如何渴盼着它，也不必讲了。

===Original===
The reader who is curious in such matters might almost reconstruct for himself (in spite of the newspaper reports which naturally are misleading) the trial of William Vane-Cartwright. He might pick out from these pages the facts capable of legal proof, which, once proved and once marshalled into their places, could leave no reasonable doubt of the prisoner’s guilt.

But, however late, the trained intelligence of the police had now been applied to the matter, and the case wore an altered aspect. No startling discovery had come to pass, only the revelation of the obvious. Some points had been ascertained which ought to have been ascertained long before; still more, facts long known had been digested, as, surely, it should have been somebody’s business to digest them from the first. In particular, tardy attention had been paid to the report of the young constable who, as I mentioned, followed Sergeant Speke into Peters’ room, and who had incurred some blame because his apparent slowness had allowed some trespassers to come and make footprints on the lawn (I fancy his notes had been overlooked when some officer in charge of the case had been superseded by another). The observed movements, just after the crime, of two or three people who were about the scene, had been set down in order. Enquiries, such as only authority could make, had ultimately been made among Vane-Cartwright’s acquaintance in the East, and though disappointing in the main, they yielded one fact of importance. Moreover, the researches which were made by Callaghan shortly after the murder, and which I had supposed at the time were so futile, now appeared in another light. Just before that suspicious flight to Paris, he had given to the police at Exeter some scrappy and ill-explained notes; and on a subsequent visit, which I have mentioned, to Scotland Yard, he had handed in a long and over-elaborate memorandum. These now received justice. I must, therefore, attempt to state, with dry accuracy, the case which was actually presented against the accused.
===Chinese===
对这类事情好奇的读者，几乎可以自行重构出威廉·韦恩-卡特赖特受审的始末（尽管报上的报道自然难免误导）。他可以从这几页文字里拣出那些能够经法律证明的事实；这些事实一经证实、一经排定各就其位，对被告的罪行便不可能再存任何合理的怀疑。

然而，警方那训练有素的才智虽姗姗来迟，如今总算用到了这桩案子上，案情也随之换了一副面目。并没有什么惊人的发现横空出世，揭开的不过是显而易见的事实。有些要点早该查明，如今总算查明了；更多的则是，早已知晓的事实终于得到了消化整理——照理说，从一开头就该有谁把消化它们当作分内之事。尤其是，我提到过的那个年轻警员的报告，终于得到了迟来的留意：他曾跟随斯皮克警佐走进彼得斯的房间，还因表面上的迟缓招来几分责难——正是那份迟缓，让几个擅入者进到草坪上踩下了脚印（我猜想，是办案主管官员换人时把他的记录给搁置了）。案发后片刻间，现场左近两三个人的举动，都已按次序记录在案。后来又做了那种唯有官方才做得成的查访，在韦恩-卡特赖特东方的旧识中进行；查访尽管大体令人失望，却也得出了一个重要事实。此外，卡拉汉在凶案后不久所做的那些调查——我当时以为何等徒劳——如今也换了一番光景。就在那次可疑的巴黎之行前，他交给埃克塞特（Exeter）警方一些零碎而语焉不详的笔记；后来又一次造访苏格兰场（此事我已提过），他呈上了一份冗长而过分铺陈的备忘录。这些材料如今总算得到了公正的对待。因此，我必须试着以干巴巴的准确，把实际用来控告被告的案情陈述一番。

===Original===
Upon the fact that he had confessed his guilt, though indeed it reversed the surface improbability that a man in his position was a criminal, I must lay no separate emphasis. Neither judge nor prosecuting counsel did so. The defence dealt with it upon a theory which turned it to positive advantage. I myself can well conceive that a man, to whom his life was little and his reputation much, might have taken the risk of a false confession to us in the hope of binding us to silence.

But, to begin, Peters was without doubt murdered on a certain night, and during that night Vane-Cartwright was one of a few who could easily have had access to him.
===Chinese===
至于他供认罪行这一事实——尽管它的确一举扭转了「他这等身份的人竟是罪犯」这一表面上的不可思议——我不必再单独着墨。法官没有着墨，控方律师也没有。辩护方则围绕它立起一套理论，反倒把它化作了于己有利的正面助力。我自己也完全能够设想：一个把性命看得轻、把名声看得重的人，为着把我们拴在沉默里，未尝不敢冒险向我们作一番虚假的供认。

但作为起点：彼得斯确凿是在某个夜里被害的，而在那一夜，能轻而易举接近他的人屈指可数，韦恩-卡特赖特正是其中之一。

===Original===
Now years before one Longhurst had disappeared; a report had got abroad that he went down in a certain ship which had been lost; the report was false; but he never reappeared; several witnesses (traced out by the enquiry of the police in the East) appeared at the trial, and swore that Vane-Cartwright had often spoken of Longhurst’s sailing in that ship; yet he must, according to Mr. Bryanston’s evidence, have known that this was false; and, according to the same evidence, he had been in Longhurst’s company after the time when the rest of Longhurst’s neighbours last saw him. From this (though the other proved facts of their connection amounted to little more than they were reputed partners) it followed that Vane-Cartwright was in a position in which suspicion of foul play towards Longhurst might easily fall on him.

Next, Peters at the time of his death not merely entertained this suspicion but was taking steps to obtain proof of its truth; for there were his letters to Bryanston and to Verschoyle still extant, and admissible in evidence as res gestae, the actual first steps which he had taken with this aim.
===Chinese===
再者，多年以前，有一个名叫朗赫斯特的人失踪了；外间流传一种说法，说他随某艘失事的船沉了下去；这说法是假的；可他从此再没有露面。几名证人（由警方在东方的查访寻访出来）到庭作证，誓称韦恩-卡特赖特屡屡说起朗赫斯特搭乘那条船航行；然而依照布莱恩斯顿先生的证词，他必定明知此说不实；而且据同一份证词，在朗赫斯特的其他乡邻最后一次见到他之后，韦恩-卡特赖特还曾与他相处过。由此（尽管两人关系经证实的部分，无非是外传的合伙经营，此外别无更多）便可断定：韦恩-卡特赖特正处在一个境地，极易让「对朗赫斯特下了毒手」的嫌疑落到自己头上。

其次，彼得斯遇害之时，不仅怀着这份疑心，还正采取措施，以求查明它是否属实；因为他写给布莱恩斯顿和弗肖伊尔的信件俱在，并作为「事实经过」（res gestae）获准采信为证据——那些信正是他抱着这一目的迈出的实际头几步。

===Original===
Next, Vane-Cartwright knew of Peters’ suspicion and was greatly perturbed by the knowledge. His whole conduct was in this regard most significant. Callaghan showed that on the first evening when he had seen the two men together their intercourse had at first been easy, but that by the end of the evening something had happened which completely altered their manners; the one became abstracted and aloof, the other eagerly watched him. Of the talk which caused this change Callaghan had only caught Peters’ question, “sailed in what,” but it was evident now to what that question referred. It was in itself strange that after this Vane-Cartwright should have availed himself of a general invitation given by Peters earlier, and have come rather suddenly to his house, putting off (as it was now shown) for that purpose a previous important engagement. It was a sinister fact that, before he did so, he had set on foot mysterious enquiries, some of which related to the two men to whom, in his presence, Peters had written letters about the affair of Longhurst, while the rest, though less obviously, appeared to be connected with the same matter. The first fruits of these enquiries (and they were telling) had been, by his arrangement, brought to him on the very afternoon before the murder. After the murder he had, it now seemed plain, stayed on at my house merely in the hope of intercepting Bryanston’s answer. By what means he knew that the sting of Dr. Verschoyle lay in his journals cannot be conjectured, but there was no mistaking the purpose with which, a little later, he obtained these journals by deceit. Altogether his conduct had been that of a man in whom Peters had aroused an anxiety so intense as to form a possible motive for murdering him.
===Chinese===
再次，韦恩-卡特赖特知道彼得斯的疑心，并为此深感惶惶不安。单在这件事上，他的全部行迹就意味深长。卡拉汉证明，他初见那两人同桌的当晚，两人起初过从甚为融洽，可是夜阑将尽时，出了什么事，把两人的神态彻底改变：一个变得心不在焉、冷若冰霜，另一个则急切地窥伺着他。至于引起这场变故的那番谈话，卡拉汉只捕捉到彼得斯的一句问话——「乘的什么船」——而这句话所指为何，如今已显而易见。经此一事，韦恩-卡特赖特竟还借彼得斯先前一句泛泛的邀约，相当突然地登门小住，并为此（如今已查明）推掉了先前一桩要紧的约会，这本身就蹊跷。更其阴森的是，成行之前，他已着手进行若干神秘的查访，其中一部分指向彼得斯当着他的面就朗赫斯特之事写信去的那两个人，其余的虽不那么显眼，看来也与同一桩事相牵连。这些查访的头一批成果（而成果非同小可）经他安排，恰在凶案前一天下午送到他手上。凶案之后，如今看来已很明白，他一直赖在我家中不走，无非指望截下布莱恩斯顿的回信。至于他凭什么知道弗肖伊尔博士的要害系于他的日志，无从悬揣；但不消多久他便使诈把那些日志弄到了手，那份用意，是错认不了的。总而言之，他的行迹，活脱脱是一个被彼得斯搅起了极度忧惧的人——忧惧之烈，足以构成杀害彼得斯的一个可能的动机。

===Original===
And altogether his conduct after the murder bore, now that it could be fully traced, the flagrant aspect of guilt. He had unlatched the window; this was now certain, though of course of that act by itself an innocent account might be given. The reader knows too the whole course of his action in regard to Trethewy and his family, beginning with the lie, which made him appear as screening Trethewy when in fact he was plotting his undoing, and ending with his breaking in upon my talk with Ellen Trethewy, who had stood where she might have seen him making those tracks in the snow. The making of the tracks﻿—this, of course, was the key to his whole conduct, the one thing, which, if quite certain, admitted of but one explanation. Only just here, when last we dealt with that matter, a faint haze still hung. Thalberg swore to having seen him in the field, where those tracks and no others were just afterwards found; Ellen Trethewy had seen him start to go there and again seen him returning. Yet, though the two corroborated each other, there might be some doubt of the inference to be drawn from what Ellen Trethewy saw (that depended on knowledge of the ground), and of the correctness of the observation made by Thalberg from afar. After all, was it absolutely impossible that Trethewy had through some strange impulse, rational or irrational, made those tracks himself﻿—perhaps, with his sense of guilt and in the overrefinement of half-drunken cunning, he had fabricated against himself a case which he thought he could break down.
===Chinese===
再者，他凶案之后的行迹，如今既已能全盘追索，赫然便是一副罪人的面目。窗闩是他打开的；这一点如今已成定论——尽管单就那一举本身，自然也不妨给出一个清白的说法。读者也知道他对特雷瑟维及其一家所作所为的全过程：始于那个谎言——它使他看似在回护特雷瑟维，实则是在密谋毁掉他；终于他闯进来打断我与埃伦·特雷瑟维的谈话——而她当时所站的位置，本可以看见他在雪地里踩下那些脚印。踩出那些脚印——这自然是解开他全部行迹的钥匙，是一旦确凿便只容一种解释的东西。只不过，在上一回我们处理这件事时，那里还悬着一层薄雾。塔尔贝格宣誓作证，说亲眼看见他在那片地里，而事后恰恰在那里发现了那些脚印，别无其他；埃伦·特雷瑟维看见他动身往那边去，随后又看见他回来。然而，尽管两人互相印证，从埃伦·特雷瑟维所见推得的结论（那有赖于对地形的老熟），以及塔尔贝格远远观察所得是否准确，仍可能存疑。毕竟，特雷瑟维会不会出于某种奇怪的冲动——或合乎理性，或不合理性——亲自踩下了那些脚印，这难道是绝对不可能的事吗？说不定，正是怀着那份负罪感，在半醉的狡狞磨得过分精细之时，他给自己捏造了一桩自以为还能推翻的罪案。

===Original===
But here the late revealed evidence came in. It was certain, first, that those tracks did not exist in the morning. The constable who had let the trespassers come in stopped them when he found them, and noted carefully how far they had gone; he got one of them, an enterprising young journalist, to verify his observation, and it resulted in this, that the part of the lawn where those guilty tracks began was absolutely untrodden then. Next it was certain now that throughout the time when those tracks were made Trethewy had been in his house. Now, when the whole course of events that morning was considered, there could be no doubt that those tracks were made by someone who knew exactly what the situation was. Since it was not Trethewy, it lay between Sergeant Speke, myself, Callaghan and Vane-Cartwright. Sergeant Speke and I could easily give account of our time that day, but I think I mentioned that there had arisen some doubt as to where Callaghan had been just at the critical hour. It was explained now; Callaghan had been too far away; just at that time he had gone again to the hotel, moved by one of his restless impulses to try and spy upon Thalberg. It lay then beyond doubt that the tracks were made by Vane-Cartwright, and it was beyond doubt why he made them.
===Chinese===
但正是在这里，迟来揭晓的证据插了进来。第一，那些脚印在早晨还不存在，这一点已确凿无疑。放擅入者进来的那个警员，发现他们时便把他们拦住，仔细记下了他们走到了多远；他还请其中一人——一个敢闯敢干的年轻记者——核证了他的观察，结果查明：草坪上那罪迹脚印起头之处，当时绝无人践踏过。第二，如今也已确定，在那些脚印踩出的整段时间里，特雷瑟维一直待在自己家中。这样，把那天早晨的全部经过放在一起掂量，便毫无疑义：那些脚印出自一个对当时局面了如指掌的人之手。既然不是特雷瑟维，嫌疑便落在斯皮克警佐、我、卡拉汉与韦恩-卡特赖特四人之间。斯皮克警佐和我都不难交代自己那天的时间去向，但我想前面提过，恰在紧要的那个时辰卡拉汉身在何处，一度起过疑议。如今这点也澄清了：卡拉汉当时离得太远；恰恰在那个时辰，他又一次被那种坐立不安的冲动支使，跑到旅馆去设法窥探塔尔贝格了。于是，那些脚印出自韦恩-卡特赖特之足，已不容置疑；他为什么踩出那些脚印，同样不容置疑。

===Original===
But the case did not rest there. The front door of Grenvile Combe had been bolted on the inside that night, before Peters died. Presumably Peters did it; anyway Vane-Cartwright and Callaghan, as they had said next morning, found it bolted when they came down disturbed by the noise, and themselves bolted it again; and Peters was then living, for they heard him in his room. The other doors had been bolted in like manner by the servants. Every window but two had also been latched. The doors had remained bolted till the servants were about in the morning, when Peters must have been some hours dead. The fastened windows were still fastened when we came to the house (a window in the back servants’ quarters had been open for a short while in the morning, but the servants had been about all the time), for the constable, before he obeyed the Sergeant and began his search outside, had been in every room and noticed every fastening. The two exceptions were Vane-Cartwright’s own open window, which did not matter, and the little window at the back, already named as a possible means of entrance. Careful experiment had now been made (Callaghan had long ago suggested it), and it showed that, whoever could climb to that window, only an infant could pass through it. No one then had entered the house by night, or, if he had previously entered it, had escaped by night; and it was also certain that no one could have lurked there concealed in the morning. Therefore, Peters was murdered by an inmate of the house, by the housemaid, or by the cook, or by Vane-Cartwright, or by Callaghan. Now the housemaid and the cook had passed a wakeful night; the disturbance in the road had aroused them and left them agitated and alarmed; each was therefore able to swear that the other had remained all night in the bedroom which they shared. Therefore, Peters was murdered either by Vane-Cartwright or by Callaghan.
===Chinese===
但案情并不止于此。案发当夜，在彼得斯死去之前，格伦维尔库姆的前门从里面上了闩。多半是彼得斯亲手闩的；无论如何，正如韦恩-卡特赖特和卡拉汉次日早晨所说，他们被响动惊起下楼时，发现门已闩上，他们便又亲手把门重新闩好；而那时彼得斯还活着，因为他们听得见他房里的动静。其余各道门也都由仆役们照样闩上了。所有窗户除两扇外也都扣上了窗闩。各道门一直闩到清晨仆役们起身走动，而那时彼得斯必然已死去几个钟头。我们赶到宅子时，闩住的窗仍然闩着（后头仆役下房有一扇窗早晨开过一小会儿，但仆役们自始至终都在左近走动），因为那个警员在听命警佐、开始户外搜寻之前，已走遍每个房间，留意过每一处门窗扣闩。例外的两扇，一是韦恩-卡特赖特自己那扇敞开的窗，无关紧要；二是屋后那扇小窗，前文已说过它是可能的入口。如今已做过细致的实验（卡拉汉早就提议过），结果表明：无论何人能攀到那扇窗前，能钻过窗去的却只有婴孩。那么，夜里没有任何人进过这座宅子；纵使有人更早潜入，夜里也无人逃出；同样可以确定，早晨也没有任何人能藏匿在宅中。因此，彼得斯死于宅内之人之手——或是女仆，或是厨娘，或是韦恩-卡特赖特，或是卡拉汉。而女仆和厨娘那一夜都不曾安枕；路上的骚动惊醒了她们，使她们心慌意乱、惶恐不安；于是两人各自都能起誓，证明对方整夜都待在两人合住的那间卧房里。因此，杀害彼得斯的，不是韦恩-卡特赖特，便是卡拉汉。

===Original===
And why not, it might be asked, by Callaghan, against whom at one time such good grounds of suspicion were to be found? The reader must by this time have seen that the eccentric and desultory proceedings of Callaghan, even his strange whim of staying in that crime-stricken house and the silly talk with which he had put me off about his aim, had, as he once boasted to me, a method, which though odd and over-ingenious, was rational and very acute. The neglected memorandum he had made for the police was enough in itself (without his frankness under cross-examination) to set his proceedings since the murder in a clear light. Callaghan, moreover, was the lifelong friend of Peters. True it was that (as the defence scented out) he had owed Peters £2,000, and Peters’ will forgave the debt. True, but it was now proved no less true, that since that will was made the debt had been paid, and paid in a significant manner. Callaghan had first remitted to Peters £500 from India. Peters, thereupon, had sent Callaghan an acquittance of the whole debt. Callaghan’s response was an immediate payment of £250 more. And the balance, £1,250, had been paid a very few days before Peters was killed. This was what an ill-inspired cross-examination revealed, and if the guilt lay between Vane-Cartwright and Callaghan, there could be no doubt which was the criminal.
===Chinese===
或许有人要问：为什么不会是卡拉汉？不是有一度，他身上的疑点也很可观么？读者到这时想必已经看清，卡拉汉那些古怪而散漫无章的举动——甚至包括他执意留宿那座横遭凶案的宅子的怪癖，以及他就此行目的拿话敷衍我的那些傻话——正如他曾向我夸口的那样，自有其章法；那章法虽离奇、虽过于巧黠，却合乎理性，而且极为敏锐。他专为警方写下的那份备受冷落的备忘录，本身便足以（不必提他在交叉盘问之下的坦白）把他凶案之后的种种行迹照个通明。何况，卡拉汉是彼得斯终生的朋友。诚然（正如辩护方嗅出的那样），他曾欠彼得斯两千英镑，而彼得斯的遗嘱勾销了这笔债。诚然如此；可如今同样查明属实的是：自那份遗嘱立下之后，这笔债已经偿清，而且还得意味深长。卡拉汉先从印度汇给彼得斯五百英镑。彼得斯随即寄给卡拉汉一张全数清讫的收据。卡拉汉的回应，是立刻又付了二百五十英镑。至于余额一千二百五十英镑，是在彼得斯遇害前寥寥几日付清的。这便是一场失策的交叉盘问所抖露出来的东西；而罪责既只在韦恩-卡特赖特与卡拉汉二人之间，哪一个是罪犯，已无可怀疑。

===Original===
So Callaghan and I had gone through tangled enquiries and at least some perilous adventures to solve a puzzle of which the solution lay all the while at our feet, and at the feet of others.

It would be melancholy now to dwell on the daring and brilliance of the defence. No witness was called for it. It opened with a truly impressive treatment of Vane-Cartwright’s confession; and the broken state of his temperament, originally sensitive and now harassed by suspicion and persecution, was described with a tenderness of which the speaker might have seemed incapable, and which called forth for the hard man in the dock a transient glow of human sympathy. Every other part of his conduct, so far as it was admitted, was made the subject of an explanation, by itself plausible. But little was admitted.
